next, we check that the proof of lemma 6.2 goes through in the general case

proof of lemma 6.2 for stabilized setting. the proof is analogous to the general case, where we replace the dependence no  $r_{\mathrm{nat}}$  and  $r_{g_\star}$  with an upper bound on  $\widehat{\mathbf{y}}_t^{\mathrm{nat}}$ ,  $\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_t^{\mathrm{nat}}$  and  $\max \{\| \widehat{g}_{u\to y}\|_{\ell_1,\mathrm{op}},\| \widehat{g}_{u\to u}\|_{\ell_1,\mathrm{op}}\}$ . in light of the above bounds, these quantities are also  $\lesssim r_{\mathrm{nat}}$  and  $r_{g_\star}$ , up to additional constant factors, yielding the same regret bound up to constants.

lastly, we establish lemma 6.5, encompassing both the stabel and stabilized case. given that the proof is somewhat involved, we organize it in the following subsection.

# d.3.1 proof of lemma 6.4/6.4b

we bound the error in estimating natures  $y$ 's and natures  $u$ 's:

lemma 6.4b (accuracy of estimated nature's  $y$  and  $u$  and  $\eta$ ). assume condition 6.1b. then for  $t \geq n + h + 1$ , we have that

$$
\left\| \mathbf {v} _ {t} ^ {\text {n a t}} - \widehat {\mathbf {v}} _ {t} ^ {\text {n a t}} \right\| _ {2} \leq 3 r _ {\mathcal {m}} r _ {\text {n a t}} \epsilon_ {g}
$$

$$
\left\| \boldsymbol {\eta} _ {t} ^ {\text {n a t}} - \hat {\boldsymbol {\eta}} _ {t} ^ {\text {n a t}} \right\| _ {2} \leq 3 r _ {\mathcal {m}} r _ {\text {n a t}} \epsilon_ {g}
$$

proof. let us focus on  $\| \mathbf{v}_t^{\mathrm{nat}} - \widehat{\mathbf{v}}_t^{\mathrm{nat}}\| _2$ , the error bound on  $\| \pmb {\eta}_t^{\mathrm{nat}} - \widehat{\pmb{\eta}}_t^{\mathrm{nat}}\| _2$  is similar. let us use the notation  $g^{[0:\ell ]}$  to denote the restriction of a markov operator to  $(g^{[i]})_{i = 0}^{\ell}$ , and  $g^{[> \ell ]}$  to restrict to  $(g^{[i]})_{i > \ell}$ . we can then bound:

$$
\begin{array}{l} \| \mathbf {v} _ {t} ^ {\mathrm {n a t}} - \widehat {\mathbf {v}} _ {t} ^ {\mathrm {n a t}} \| _ {2} = \left\| \sum_ {s = 1} ^ {t} g _ {\mathrm {e x} \rightarrow (y, u)} ^ {[ t - s ]} \mathbf {u} _ {t} ^ {\mathrm {e x , a l g}} - \widehat {g} _ {\mathrm {e x} \rightarrow (y, u)} ^ {[ t - s ]} \mathbf {u} _ {t} ^ {\mathrm {e x , a l g}} \right\| _ {2} \\ \leq \left\| g _ {\mathrm {e x} \rightarrow (y, u)} ^ {[ 0: t - (n + 1) ]} - \widehat {g} _ {\mathrm {e x} \rightarrow (y, u)} ^ {[ 0: t - (n + 1) ]} \right\| _ {\ell_ {1}, \mathrm {o p}} \cdot \max  _ {s \in [ n + 1: t ]} \| \mathbf {u} _ {s} ^ {\mathrm {e x}, \mathrm {a l g}} \| \\ + \left\| g _ {\mathrm {e x} \rightarrow (y, u)} ^ {[ > t - (n + 1) ]} - \widehat {g} _ {\mathrm {e x} \rightarrow (y, u)} ^ {[ > t - n + 1 ]} \right\| _ {\ell_ {1}, \mathrm {o p}} \cdot \max  _ {s \leq n} \| \mathbf {u} _ {s} ^ {\mathrm {e x , a l g}} \|. \\ \end{array}
$$

for  $t \geq n + h - 1$ , we have  $\| \mathbf{u}_t^{\mathrm{alg}} \| \leq 2r_{\mathcal{m}} r_{\mathrm{nat}}$ , and under condition 6.1b, we have  $\max_{s \leq n} \| \mathbf{u}_s^{\mathrm{ex}, \mathrm{alg}} \| \leq \overline{r}_{\mathbf{u}}$ . moreover, we can bound  $\left\| g_{\mathrm{ex} \to (y, u)}^{[0:t - n + 1]} - \widehat{g}_{\mathrm{ex} \to (y, u)}^{[0:t - n + 1]} \right\|_{\ell_1, \mathrm{op}} \leq \left\| g_{\mathrm{ex} \to (y, u)} - \widehat{g}_{\mathrm{ex} \to (y, u)} \right\|_{\ell_1, \mathrm{op}} \leq \epsilon_g$  under condition 6.1b. in addition, since  $t \geq n + h + 1$ ,  $t - n + 1 \geq h$ , so

$$
\begin{array}{l} \left\| g _ {\mathrm {e x} \rightarrow (y, u)} ^ {[ > t - (n + 1) ]} - \widehat {g} _ {\mathrm {e x} \rightarrow (y, u)} ^ {[ > t - n + 1 ]} \right\| _ {\ell_ {1}, \mathrm {o p}} \leq \left\| g _ {\mathrm {e x} \rightarrow (y, u)} ^ {[ > h ]} - \widehat {g} _ {\mathrm {e x} \rightarrow (y, u)} ^ {[ > h ]} \right\| _ {\ell_ {1}, \mathrm {o p}} \\ = \left\| g _ {\mathrm {e x} \rightarrow (y, u)} ^ {[ > h ]} \right\| _ {\ell_ {1}, \mathrm {o p}} \leq \psi_ {g _ {\star}} (h + 1), \\ \end{array}
$$

where we use the fact that  $\widehat{g}_{\mathrm{ex}\to (y,u)}^{[i]} = 0$  for  $i > 0$ , and the fact that we define  $\psi_{g_{\star}}(\cdot)\coloneqq \max \{\psi_{g_{\mathrm{ex}\to (y,u)}}(\cdot),\psi_{g_{\mathrm{ex}\to \eta}}(\cdot)\}$  (definition 2.3b). thus, all in all,

$$
\| \mathbf {v} _ {t} ^ {\mathrm {n a t}} - \widehat {\mathbf {v}} _ {t} ^ {\mathrm {n a t}} \| _ {2} \leq 2 r _ {\mathcal {m}} r _ {\mathrm {n a t}} \epsilon_ {g} + \psi_ {g _ {*}} (h + 1) r _ {\mathbf {u}, \mathrm {e s t}} \leq 3 r _ {\mathcal {m}} r _ {\mathrm {n a t}} \epsilon_ {g},
$$

where the last step holds because  $r_{\mathcal{m}}r_{\mathrm{nat}} \geq 1$  by assumption, and that under condition 6.1b,  $\psi_{g_\star}(h + 1)r_{\mathbf{u},\mathrm{est}} \leq \epsilon_g$ .

# d.3.2 proof of lemma 6.5

we prove the lemma in the more general stabilized setting, where we require the stronger condition 6.1b instead of condition 6.1. for completeness, we state this general bound here

lemma 6.5b (approximation error bounds: stabilized). under condition 6.1b,

$$
\left(\text {l o s s} \right.
$$

proof. let us start with the loss approximation error. for  $t \geq n + h + 1$ , and using  $\mathbf{u}_t^{\mathrm{ex}}(\mathbf{m}_t) = \mathbf{u}_t^{\mathrm{ex,alg}}$ , we have

$$
\begin{array}{l} \left\| \mathbf {v} _ {t} ^ {\mathrm {a l g}} - \mathbf {v} _ {t} [ \mathbf {m} _ {t: t - h} \mid \widehat {g} _ {\mathrm {e x} \rightarrow (y, u)}, \widehat {\boldsymbol {\eta}} _ {1: t} ^ {\mathrm {n a t}}, \widehat {\mathbf {v}} _ {t} ^ {\mathrm {n a t}} ] \right\| _ {2} \\ = \| \mathbf {v} _ {t} ^ {\mathrm {n a t}} + \sum_ {s = 1} ^ {t} g _ {\mathrm {e x} \rightarrow (y, u)} ^ {[ t - s ]} \mathbf {u} _ {s} ^ {\mathrm {e x , a l g}} - (\widehat {\mathbf {v}} _ {t} ^ {\mathrm {n a t}} + \sum_ {s = t - h} ^ {t} \widehat {g} _ {\mathrm {e x} \rightarrow (y, u)} ^ {[ t - s ]} \mathbf {u} _ {s} ^ {\mathrm {e x , a l g}}) \| _ {2} \\ = \| \mathbf {v} _ {t} ^ {\mathrm {n a t}} + \sum_ {s = 1} ^ {t} g _ {\mathrm {e x} \rightarrow (y, u)} ^ {[ t - s ]} \mathbf {u} _ {s} ^ {\mathrm {e x , a l g}} - (\widehat {\mathbf {v}} _ {t} ^ {\mathrm {n a t}} + \sum_ {s = 1} ^ {t} \widehat {g} _ {\mathrm {e x} \rightarrow (y, u)} ^ {[ t - s ]} \mathbf {u} _ {s} ^ {\mathrm {e x , a l g}}) \| _ {2} \quad \quad \quad \widehat {g} _ {\mathrm {e x} \rightarrow (y, u)} ^ {[ i ]} = 0 \text {f o r} i > h \\ \leq \| \mathbf {y} _ {t} ^ {\text {n a t}} - \widehat {\mathbf {y}} _ {t} ^ {\text {n a t}} \| _ {2} + \left\| \sum_ {s = 1} ^ {t} g _ {\mathrm {e x} \rightarrow (y, u)} ^ {[ t - s ]} \mathbf {u} _ {t} ^ {\mathrm {e x}, \mathrm {a l g}} - \widehat {g} _ {\mathrm {e x} \rightarrow (y, u)} ^ {[ t - s ]} \mathbf {u} _ {t} ^ {\mathrm {e x}, \mathrm {a l g}} \right\| _ {2} \\ = \| \mathbf {v} _ {t} ^ {\mathrm {n a t}} - \widehat {\mathbf {v}} _ {t} ^ {\mathrm {n a t}} \| _ {2} + \| \mathbf {v} _ {t} ^ {\mathrm {n a t}} = \widehat {\mathbf {v}} _ {t} ^ {\mathrm {n a t}} \| _ {2} \leq 6 r _ {\mathcal {m}} r _ {\mathrm {n a t}} \epsilon_ {g} \\ \end{array}
$$

where we use lemma 6.1b in the last inequality. moreover, recalling the following bound from lemma d.5,

$$
\left\| \mathbf {v} _ {t} \left[ \mathbf {m} _ {t: t - h} \mid \widehat {g} _ {\mathrm {e x} \rightarrow (y, u)}, \widehat {\boldsymbol {\eta}} _ {1: t} ^ {\mathrm {n a t}}, \widehat {\mathbf {v}} _ {t} ^ {\mathrm {n a t}} \right]\right\| _ {2} \leq 6 r _ {g _ {\star}} r _ {\mathcal {m}} r _ {\mathrm {n a t}}
$$

we have

$$
\begin{array}{l} \left. \max  \left\{\| \mathbf {v} _ {t} ^ {\mathrm {a l g}} \| _ {2}, \left\| \mathbf {v} _ {t} \left[ m _ {t: t - h} \mid \widehat {g} _ {\mathrm {e x} \rightarrow (y, u)}, \widehat {\eta} _ {1: t} ^ {\mathrm {n a t}}, \widehat {\mathbf {v}} _ {t} ^ {\mathrm {n a t}} \right]\right\| _ {2} \right\}\right. \\ \leq 6 r _ {g _ {\star}} r _ {\mathcal {m}} r _ {\text {n a t}} + 6 r _ {\mathcal {m}} r _ {\text {n a t}} \epsilon_ {g} \leq 9 r _ {g _ {\star}} r _ {\mathcal {m}} r _ {\text {n a t}}, \\ \end{array}
$$

where we use the bound  $\epsilon_g\leq 1 / 2r_{\mathcal{m}}r_{g_\star}\leq r_{g_\star} / 2$  under condition 6.1b and the bounds  $r_{\mathcal{m}},r_{g_{\star}}\geq 1$  hence, we have

(loss approximation error)

$$
\begin{array}{l} := \sum_ {t = n + m + h + 1} ^ {t} \ell_ {t} \left(\mathbf {v} _ {t} ^ {\mathrm {a l g}}\right) - \sum_ {t = n + m + h + 1} ^ {t} f _ {t} \left[ \mathbf {m} _ {t: t - h} \mid \widehat {g} _ {\mathrm {e x} \rightarrow (y, u)}, \widehat {\boldsymbol {\eta}} _ {1: t} ^ {\mathrm {n a t}}, \widehat {\mathbf {v}} _ {t} ^ {\mathrm {n a t}} \right] \\ \leq \sum_ {t = n + m + h + 1} ^ {t} | \ell_ {t} (\mathbf {y} _ {t} ^ {\mathrm {a l g}}, \mathbf {u} _ {t} ^ {\mathrm {a l g}}) - \ell_ {t} (\mathbf {v} _ {t} [ \mathbf {m} _ {t: t - h} \mid \widehat {g} _ {\mathrm {e x} \rightarrow (y, u)}, \widehat {\eta} _ {1: t} ^ {\mathrm {n a t}}, \widehat {\mathbf {v}} _ {t} ^ {\mathrm {n a t}} ]) ] | \\ \leq 9 l r _ {g _ {\star}} r _ {\mathcal {m}} r _ {\mathrm {n a t}} \cdot \sum_ {t = n + m + h + 1} ^ {t} \left\| \mathbf {v} _ {t} ^ {\mathrm {a l g}} - \mathbf {v} _ {t} [ \mathbf {m} _ {t: t - h} \mid \widehat {g} _ {\mathrm {e x} \rightarrow (y, u)}, \widehat {\boldsymbol {\eta}} _ {1: t} ^ {\mathrm {n a t}}, \widehat {\mathbf {v}} _ {t} ^ {\mathrm {n a t}} ] \right\| _ {2} \\ \leq 5 4 l t r _ {g _ {\star}} r _ {\mathcal {m}} ^ {2} r _ {\mathrm {n a t}} ^ {2} \epsilon_ {g}. \\ \end{array}
$$

where we used assumption 2 and the bounds computed above.

let us now turn to the comparator approximation error

(comparator approximation error)

$$
\begin{array}{l} := \inf  _ {m \in \mathcal {m}} \sum_ {t = n + m + h + 1} ^ {t} f _ {t} (m \mid \widehat {g} _ {\mathrm {e x} \rightarrow (y, u)}, \widehat {\boldsymbol {\eta}} _ {1: t} ^ {\mathrm {n a t}}, \widehat {\mathbf {v}} _ {t} ^ {\mathrm {n a t}}) - \inf  _ {m \in \mathcal {m}} \sum_ {t = n + m + h + 1} ^ {t} \ell_ {t} (\mathbf {y} _ {t} ^ {m}, \mathbf {u} _ {t} ^ {m}) \\ \leq \max _ {m \in \mathcal {m}} \sum_ {t = n + m + h + 1} ^ {t} | \ell_ {t} (\mathbf {v} _ {t} ^ {m}) - \ell_ {t} (\mathbf {v} _ {t} (m | \widehat {g} _ {\mathrm {e x} \rightarrow (y, u)}, \widehat {\boldsymbol {\eta}} _ {1: t} ^ {\mathrm {n a t}}, \widehat {\mathbf {v}} _ {t} ^ {\mathrm {n a t}})) | \\ \leq 6 l t r _ {g _ {\star}} r _ {\mathcal {m}} r _ {\mathrm {n a t}} \max _ {m \in \mathcal {m}} \sum_ {t = n + m + h + 1} ^ {t} \left\| \mathbf {v} _ {t} ^ {m} - \mathbf {v} _ {t} (m \mid \widehat {g} _ {\mathrm {e x} \rightarrow (y, u)}, \widehat {\boldsymbol {\eta}} _ {1: t} ^ {\mathrm {n a t}}, \widehat {\mathbf {v}} _ {t} ^ {\mathrm {n a t}}) \right\| _ {2} \\ \end{array}
$$

where again we use the magnitude bounds in lemmas 5.1b and d.5, and the lipschitz assumption (assumption 2). let us bound the differences between the  $\mathbf{v}_t$  terms, taking care that errors is introduced by both the approximation of the transfer function  $g_{\mathrm{ex}\to (y,u)}$  and the nature's  $\eta$  sequence  $\pmb{\eta}_{t}^{\mathrm{nat}}$ . for  $t\geq n + h + 1 + m$ , we obtain

$$
\begin{array}{l} \left\| \mathbf {v} _ {t} ^ {m} - \mathbf {v} _ {t} (m \mid \widehat {g} _ {\mathrm {e x} \rightarrow (y, u)}, \widehat {\eta} _ {1: t} ^ {\mathrm {n a t}}, \widehat {\mathbf {v}} _ {t} ^ {\mathrm {n a t}}) \right\| _ {2} \\ = \left\| \mathbf {v} _ {t} ^ {\mathrm {n a t}} + \sum_ {s = 1} ^ {t} g _ {\mathrm {e x} \rightarrow (y, u)} ^ {[ t - s ]} \mathbf {u} _ {s} ^ {\mathrm {e x}} (m \mid \boldsymbol {\eta} _ {1: s} ^ {\mathrm {n a t}}) - \left(\widehat {\mathbf {v}} _ {t} ^ {\mathrm {n a t}} + \sum_ {s = t - h} ^ {t} \widehat {g} _ {\mathrm {e x} \rightarrow (y, u)} ^ {[ t - s ]} \mathbf {u} _ {s} ^ {\mathrm {e x}} (m \mid \widehat {\boldsymbol {\eta}} _ {1: s} ^ {\mathrm {n a t}})\right)\right\| _ {2} \\ = \underbrace {\| \mathbf {v} _ {t} ^ {\text {n a t}} - \widehat {\mathbf {v}} _ {t} ^ {\text {n a t}} \| _ {2}} _ {\leq 3 r _ {\mathcal {m}} r _ {\text {n a t}} \epsilon_ {g}} + \underbrace {\| g _ {\text {e x} \rightarrow (y , u)} - \widehat {g} _ {\text {e x} \rightarrow (y , u)} \| _ {\ell_ {1} , \text {o p}}} _ {\leq \epsilon_ {g}} \underbrace {\max  _ {t - h \leq s \leq t} \| \mathbf {u} _ {s} ^ {\text {e x}} (m \mid \widehat {\boldsymbol {\eta}} _ {1 : s} ^ {\text {n a t}}) \|} _ {\leq 2 r _ {\mathcal {m}} r _ {\text {n a t}}} \\ + \left\| \sum_ {s = 1} ^ {t - h + 1} g _ {\mathrm {e x} \rightarrow (y, u)} ^ {[ t - s ]} \mathbf {u} _ {s} ^ {\mathrm {e x}} (m \mid \widehat {\boldsymbol {\eta}} _ {1: s} ^ {\mathrm {n a t}}) \right\| _ {2} \\ = 5 r _ {\mathcal {m}} r _ {\mathrm {n a t}} \epsilon_ {g} + \left\| \sum_ {s = 1} ^ {t} g _ {\mathrm {e x} \rightarrow (y, u)} ^ {[ t - s ]} \mathbf {u} _ {s} ^ {\mathrm {e x}} (m \mid \widehat {\boldsymbol {\eta}} _ {1: s} ^ {\mathrm {n a t}}) \right\| _ {2}. \\ \end{array}
$$

where we have used the magnitude bounds in lemma 6.1b and d.5. we can further bound

$$
\begin{array}{l} \left\| \sum_ {s = 1} ^ {t} g _ {\mathrm {e x} \rightarrow (y, u)} ^ {[ t - s ]} \mathbf {u} _ {s} ^ {\mathrm {e x}} (m \mid \widehat {\boldsymbol {\eta}} _ {1: s} ^ {\mathrm {n a t}}) \right\| _ {2} \leq \psi_ {g _ {*}} (h + 1) \max  _ {s \leq t} \| \mathbf {u} _ {s} ^ {\mathrm {e x}} (m \mid \widehat {\boldsymbol {\eta}} _ {1: s} ^ {\mathrm {n a t}} \| \\ \leq \psi_ {g _ {*}} (h + 1) 2 \max  \left\{r _ {\mathbf {u}, \text {e s t}}, r _ {\mathcal {m}} r _ {\text {n a t}} \right\}, \\ \end{array}
$$

where we use lemma 6.1b above. from conditions 6.1/6.1b, we can bound  $\psi_{g_\star}(h + 1)r_{\mathbf{u},\mathrm{est}}\leq \epsilon_g$ . and since  $r_{\mathbf{u},\mathrm{est}}\geq 1$ , this implies that the above is at most  $2\psi_{g_\star}r_{\mathbf{u},\mathrm{est}}r_{\mathcal{m}}r_{\mathrm{nat}}\leq 2\epsilon_gr_{\mathcal{m}}r_{\mathrm{nat}}$ . thus, from the above previous two displays,

$$
\left\| \mathbf {v} _ {t} ^ {m} - \mathbf {v} _ {t} (m \mid \widehat {g} _ {\mathrm {e x} \rightarrow (y, u)}, \widehat {\eta} _ {1: t} ^ {\mathrm {n a t}}, \widehat {\mathbf {v}} _ {t} ^ {\mathrm {n a t}}) \right\| _ {2} \leq 7 \epsilon_ {g} r _ {\mathcal {m}} r _ {\mathrm {n a t}},
$$

giving

$$
(c o m p a r a t o r) \leq 6 l r _ {g, *} r _ {\mathcal {m}} r _ {\text {n a t}} \cdot 7 \epsilon_ {g} r _ {\mathcal {m}} r _ {\text {n a t}} = 4 2 l t r _ {g, *} r _ {\mathcal {m}} ^ {2} r _ {\text {n a t}} ^ {2} \epsilon_ {g}
$$

combining the two bounds, we and using  $r_{g_{\star}}, r_{\mathcal{m}} \geq 1$

$$
(\mathrm {c o m p a r a t o r a p p r o x i m a t i o n e r r o r}) + (\mathrm {l o s s a p p r o x i m a t i o n e r r o r}) \lesssim t l r _ {g _ {\star}} r _ {\mathcal {m}} ^ {2} r _ {\mathrm {n a t}} ^ {2} \epsilon_ {g}
$$

# e strongly convex, semi-adversarial regret

we begin by stating a slight generalization of the semi-adversarial model described by assumption 6. recall the assumption that our noises decompose as follows:

$$
\mathbf {w} _ {t} = \mathbf {w} _ {t} ^ {\mathrm {a d v}} + \mathbf {w} _ {t} ^ {\mathrm {s t o c h}}
$$

$$
\mathbf {e} _ {t} = \mathbf {e} _ {t} ^ {\mathrm {a d v}} + \mathbf {e} _ {t} ^ {\mathrm {s t o c h}}
$$

we make the following assumption on the noise and losses:

assumption 6b (semi-adversarial noise: martingale structure). we assume that there is a filtration  $(\mathcal{f}_t)_{t\geq 0}$  and a matrix  $\sigma_{\mathrm{noise}}\in \mathbb{r}^{(d_x + d_y)^2}\succeq 0$  (possibly degenerate), and  $\sigma_{\mathbf{w}}^2,\sigma_{\mathbf{e}}^2\geq 0$  (possibly zero) such that the following hold:

1. the adversarial disturbance sequences  $(\mathbf{w}_t^{\mathrm{adv}})$  and  $(\mathbf{e}_t^{\mathrm{adv}})$  and the loss sequence  $\ell_t(\cdot)$  are oblivious, in the sense that they are  $\mathcal{f}_0$ -adapted.  
2. the sequences  $(\mathbf{w}_t^{\mathrm{stoch}})$  and  $(\mathbf{e}_t^{\mathrm{stoch}})$  and  $(\mathcal{f}_t)$ -adapted  
3.  $\mathbb{e}[\mathbf{e}_t^{\mathrm{stoch}}\mid \mathcal{f}_{t - 1}] = 0$ $\mathbb{e}[\mathbf{w}_t^{\mathrm{stoch}}\mid \mathcal{f}_{t - 1}] = 0$  
4. the noises satisfy

$$
\mathbb {e} \left[ \left[ \begin{array}{c} \mathbf {w} _ {t} ^ {\mathrm {s t o c h}} \\ \mathbf {e} _ {t} ^ {\mathrm {s t o c h}} \end{array} \right] \left[ \begin{array}{c} \mathbf {w} _ {t} ^ {\mathrm {s t o c h}} \\ \mathbf {e} _ {t} ^ {\mathrm {s t o c h}} \end{array} \right] ^ {\top} | \mathcal {f} _ {t - 1} \right] \succeq \sigma_ {\mathrm {n o i s e}} \succeq \left[ \begin{array}{c c} \sigma_ {\mathbf {w}} ^ {2} i _ {d _ {x}} & 0 \\ 0 & \sigma_ {\mathbf {e}} ^ {2} i _ {d _ {y}} \end{array} \right].
$$

moreover, at least one of the following hold:

(a) the system is internally stable has no stabilizing controller  $\pi_0$ , and  $\sigma_{\mathbf{w}}^2 + \sigma_{\mathbf{e}}^2 > 0$  
(b) the system is stabilized by a static feedback controller  $\pi_0$  (that is,  $a_{\pi_0} = 0$  and  $\dim (\mathbf{z}^{\pi}) = 0$ ), and  $\sigma_{\mathbf{w}}^{2} > 0$  
(c) the system is stabilized by a general stabilizing controller, and  $\sigma_{\mathbf{e}}^{2} > 0$  11

as in the stable setting, the strong convexity parameter governs the functions

$$
f _ {t; k} \left(m \mid g _ {\star}, \mathbf {y} _ {1: t} ^ {\text {n a t}}, \mathbf {u} _ {1: t} ^ {\text {n a t}}\right) := \mathbb {e} \left[ f _ {t} \left(m \mid g _ {\star}, \mathbf {y} _ {1: t} ^ {\text {n a t}}, \mathbf {u} _ {1: t} ^ {\text {n a t}}\right) \mid \mathcal {f} _ {t - k} \right]. \tag {e.1}
$$

for stabilized settings, proposition 7.1 admits the following generalization:

proposition 7.1b (strong convexity for known system). suppose that we interact with an internally-controlled system (definition 2.1). then, under assumptions assumptions 2, 3, 5, 1b and 6b, there exists system dependent constants  $m_{\mathrm{sys}}, p_{\mathrm{sys}} \geq 0$  and  $\alpha_{f;0} > 0$  such that, for  $h = \lfloor m / 3 \rfloor$ ,  $k = m + 2h$ , and  $m \geq m_{\mathrm{sys}}$ , the functions  $f_{t;k}(m \mid g_{\star}, \mathbf{y}_{1:t}^{\mathrm{nat}}, \mathbf{u}_{1:t}^{\mathrm{nat}})$  are  $\alpha_{f;m}$ -strongly convex, where

$$
\alpha_ {f; m} := \alpha_ {\mathrm {l o s s}} \cdot \alpha_ {\mathrm {s y s}} \cdot m ^ {p _ {\mathrm {s y s}}}
$$

in other words, the strong convexity parameter decays at most polynomially in  $m$ .

the above proposition is given in appendix f.2.1. for general ldc-ex controllers, we do not have transparent expressions for  $\alpha_{\mathrm{sys}}$  and  $m^{p_{\mathrm{sys}}}$ . nevertheless, we ensure that the above bound is strong enough to ensure rates of  $\log^{\mathcal{o}(1) + p_{\mathrm{sys}}}t$  and  $\sqrt{t}\log^{\mathcal{o}(1) + p_{\mathrm{sys}}}$ , where the exponent hidden by  $\mathcal{o}(1)$  does not depend on system parameters (so that the exponents are determined solely by  $p_{\mathrm{sys}}$ ). we make a couple remarks, which in particular describe how  $p_{\mathrm{sys}}$  is often 0 in many settings:

1. in general, the strong convexity parameters of the system are determined by the properties of the z-transforms for relevant operators. a general expression is given in theorem 11, and the preliminaries and definitions relevant for the theorem are given in appendix f.1. proposition 7.1b is proven in appendix f.2 as a consequence of this more general result, and appendix f contains all details related to establishing strong convexity.  
2. in appendix f.2.2, we show that for systems stabilized via static feedback, we can take  $p_{\mathrm{sys}} = 0$ , and give explicit and transparent bounds on  $\alpha_{\mathrm{sys}}$ . this recovers the special case of internally stable systems as a special case, where we can take  $\alpha_{\mathrm{sys}} = \sigma_{\mathbf{e}}^{2} + \frac{\sigma_{\mathbf{w}}^{2}\sigma_{\min}(c_{\star})}{(1 + \|a_{\star}\|_{\mathrm{op}})^{2}}$ .  
3. for the special case of internally systems (proposition 7.1.), we present a smaller self-contained proof that does not appeal to z-transform machinery (proposition 7.1. note that this result does not require that  $m > m_{\mathrm{sys}}$  restriction required by proposition 7.1b.  
4. the parameter  $m_{\mathrm{sys}}$  is related to the decay of the system, and can be deduced from the conditions of theorem 11.

theorems 4 and 5 generalize to the stabilized-system setting:

theorem 4b (fast rate for known system: stabilized case). suppose assumptions assumptions 2, 3, 5, 1b and 6b holds. then with the additional condition  $h = \lfloor m / 3 \rfloor$  and appropriate modifications as in definition d.1, theorem 4 holds verbatim when  $\alpha_f$  is replaced with the stabilized analogue  $\alpha_{f:m}$  from proposition 7.1b. in particular, taking  $\alpha = \omega(\alpha_{f:m})$ , we obtain regret bounded by

$$
\operatorname {r e g r e t} _ {t} (\psi) \lesssim \frac {l ^ {2} m ^ {3 + p _ {\text {s y s}}} d _ {\min } r _ {\text {n a t}} ^ {4} r _ {g _ {\star}} ^ {4} r _ {\mathcal {m}} ^ {2}}{\min  \left\{\alpha_ {\text {s y s}}, l r _ {\text {n a t}} ^ {2} r _ {g _ {\star}} ^ {2} \right\}} \left(1 + \frac {\beta_ {\text {l o s s}}}{l r _ {\mathcal {m}}}\right) \cdot \log \frac {t}{\delta}. \tag {e.2}
$$

in particular, under assumption 4, we obtain

$$
\operatorname {r e g r e t} _ {t} (\psi) \lesssim \left(\frac {1}{1 - \rho} \log \frac {t}{\delta}\right) ^ {p _ {\text {s y s}}} \cdot \operatorname {p o l y} (c, l, \beta_ {\text {l o s s}}, \frac {1}{\alpha}, \sigma_ {\text {n o i s e}} ^ {2}, \frac {1}{1 - \rho}, \log \frac {t}{\delta}) \cdot \left(d _ {\max } ^ {2} \sqrt {t} + d _ {\max } ^ {3}\right),
$$

where the exponents in the poly(·) term do not depend on system parameters, although  $p_{\mathrm{sys}}$  does.

again, for general stabilized system, we may suffer exponents which depend on this system-dependent  $p_{\mathrm{sys}}$ . but, as discussed above  $p_{\mathrm{sys}}$  may be equal to 0 in many cases of interest.

for unknown systems, we have the following:

theorem 5b (fast rate for unknown system: stabilized case). suppose assumptions assumptions 2, 3, 5, 1b and 6b holds. then with the additional condition  $h = \lfloor m / 3 \rfloor$  and appropriate modifications as in definition d.1, theorem 5 holds verbatim when  $\alpha_f$  is replaced with the stabilized analogoue  $\alpha_{f;m}$  from proposition 7.1b. in particular, taking  $\alpha = \omega (\alpha_{f:m})$  and assumption 4, we obtain

$$
\operatorname {r e g r e t} _ {t} (\psi) \lesssim \operatorname {p o l y} (c, l, \beta_ {\text {l o s s}}, \frac {1}{\alpha}, \sigma_ {\text {n o i s e}} ^ {2}, \frac {1}{1 - \rho}, \log \frac {t}{\delta}) \qquad \cdot (d _ {\max } ^ {2} \sqrt {t (\frac {1}{1 - \rho} \log \frac {t}{\delta}) ^ {p _ {\text {s y s}}}} + (\frac {1}{1 - \rho} \log \frac {t}{\delta}) ^ {p _ {\text {s y s}}} d _ {\max } ^ {3}),
$$

where the exponent in  $p_{\mathrm{sys}}$  where the exponents in the poly(·) term do not depend on system parameters, although  $p_{\mathrm{sys}}$  does.

# e.1 proof details for theorems 4 and 4b

the proof of theorems theorems 4 and 4b are identical, except for the difference in strong convexity parameters in view of proposition 7.1b and proposition 7.1. thus, the proof of proposition 7.1b follows from the proof of proposition 7.1b given in section 7, amending  $\alpha_{f}$  to  $\alpha_{f,m}$  where it arises.

it remains to supply a the omitted proof of the lemma that establishes smoothness of the objectives, lemma 7.1. we restate the lemma here to include the  $|g_{\star}, \mathbf{y}_{1:t}^{\mathrm{nat}}, \mathbf{u}_{1:t}^{\mathrm{nat}}$  encountered in the stabilized case:

lemma 7.1b (smoothness). the functions  $f_{t}(m \mid g_{\star}, \mathbf{y}_{1:t}^{\mathrm{nat}}, \mathbf{u}_{1:t}^{\mathrm{nat}})$  are  $\beta_{f}$ -smooth, where we define  $\beta_{f} := mr_{\mathrm{nat}}^{2}r_{g_{\star}}^{2}\beta_{\mathrm{loss}}$ .

proof. for brevity, we omit  $|g_{\mathrm{ex} \to (y,u)}, \pmb{\eta}_{1:t}^{\mathrm{nat}}, \mathbf{v}_t^{\mathrm{nat}}$ . let  $\mathsf{d}\mathbf{v}_t[\cdot]$  the differential of the function as maps from  $\mathbb{r}^{(md_yd_u)} \to \mathbb{r}^{d_y + d_u}$ , these are elements of  $\mathbb{r}^{(md_yd_u) \times (d_y + d_u)}$ . these are affine functions, and thus do not depend on the  $m$  argument. from the chain rule (with appropriate transpose conventions), and the fact that affine functions have vanishing second derivative

$$
\nabla f _ {t} (m) = \mathrm {d} \mathbf {v} _ {t} (\nabla \ell) (\mathbf {v} _ {t})
$$

$$
\nabla^ {2} f _ {t} (m) = \mathsf {d} \mathbf {v} _ {t} \cdot (\nabla^ {2} \ell) (\mathbf {v} _ {t}) \cdot \mathsf {d} \mathbf {v} _ {t} ^ {\top} \preceq \beta_ {\text {l o s s}} \| \mathsf {d} \mathbf {v} _ {t} \| _ {\text {o p}} ^ {2} i \tag {e.3}
$$

let us now bound the norm of the differentials. observe that  $\| \mathsf{d}(\mathbf{u}_t(m)\|_{\mathrm{op}}$  and  $\| \mathsf{d}(\mathbf{y}_t(m)\|_{\mathrm{op}}$  are just the frobenius norm to  $\ell_2$  lipschitz constant of  $m\mapsto \mathbf{v}_t(m)$  is bounded by  $\sqrt{m} r_{g_\star}r_{\mathrm{nat}}$  via lemma d.2. thus  $\nabla^2 f_t(m)\preceq \beta_{\mathrm{loss}}mr_{g_\star}^2 r_{\mathrm{nat}}^2 i$ , as needed.

# e.2 supporting proofs for theorems theorems 5 and 5b

we now generalize to the stabilized, unknown setting. throughout, we shall use the various magnitude bounds on  $\widehat{\mathbf{y}}^{\mathrm{nat}},\widehat{\mathbf{u}}^{\mathrm{nat}},\ldots$  developed in appendix d.3 for unknown system / lipschitz loss setting.

for this strongly convex, stabilized, unknown setting, we generalize the true prediction losses of definition 8.1 as follows:

definition 8.1b (true prediction losses). we define the true prediction losses as

$$
\begin{array}{l} \mathbf {v} _ {t} ^ {\text {p r e d}} \left[ m _ {t: t - h} \right] := \mathbf {v} _ {t} \left[ m _ {t: t - h} \mid g _ {\mathrm {e x} \rightarrow (y, u)}, \hat {\eta} _ {1: t} ^ {\text {n a t}}, \mathbf {v} _ {t} ^ {\text {n a t}} \right] \\ = \mathbf {v} _ {t} ^ {\text {n a t}} + \sum_ {i = 0} ^ {h} g _ {\mathrm {e x} \rightarrow (y, u)} ^ {[ i ]} \mathbf {u} _ {t} ^ {\text {e x}} (m \mid \widehat {\boldsymbol {\eta}} _ {1: t - i} ^ {\text {n a t}}) \\ \end{array}
$$

$$
f _ {t} ^ {\text {p r e d}} \left[ m _ {t: t - h} \right] := \ell_ {t} \left(\mathbf {v} _ {t} ^ {\text {p r e d}} \left[ m _ {t: t - h} \right]\right),
$$

and let  $f_{t}^{\mathrm{pred}}(m) = f_{t}^{\mathrm{pred}}(m,\dots ,m)$  denote the unary specialization. the corresponding conditional functions of interest are

$$
f _ {t; k} ^ {\text {p r e d}} (m) := \mathbb {e} \left[ f _ {t} ^ {\text {p r e d}} (m) \mid \mathcal {f} _ {t - k} \right].
$$

throughout the proof, it will be useful to adopt the shorthand  $\mathbf{v}_t^\star (m)\coloneqq \mathbf{u}_t(m\mid g_{\mathrm{ex}\to (y,u)},\pmb{\eta}_{1:t}^{\mathrm{nat}},\mathbf{v}_t^{\mathrm{nat}})$  and  $\mathbf{y}_t^\star (m)\coloneqq \mathbf{y}_t(m\mid g_\star ,\mathbf{y}_{1:t}^{\mathrm{nat}})$  to denote the counterfactuals for the true nature's  $y$ , and  $\hat{\mathbf{v}}_t(m)\coloneqq (m\mid$ $\widehat{g}_{\mathrm{ex}\rightarrow (y,u)},\widehat{\pmb{\eta}}_{1:t}^{\mathrm{nat}},\widehat{\mathbf{v}}_t^{\mathrm{nat}})$  denote the counterfactuals for the estimates  $\widehat{g}$  and  $\widehat{\pmb{\eta}}^{\mathrm{nat}}$  and  $\widehat{\mathbf{v}}^{\mathrm{nat}}$ . note that  $\mathbf{u}^{\mathrm{pred}}$  and  $\mathbf{y}^{\mathrm{pred}}$  can be though as interpolating between these two sequences.

we shall also let  $\mathsf{d}\mathbf{v}_t^{\mathrm{pred}}$  denote differentials as elements of are elements of  $\mathbb{r}^{(md_yd_u)\times (d_y + d_u)}$ , and similarly for  $\mathsf{d}\mathbf{v}^{\star}$  and  $\mathsf{d}\widehat{\mathbf{v}}$ . as these functions are affine, the differential is independent of  $m$ -argument

# e.2.1 preliminary notation and perturbation bounds

before continuing, we shall state and prove two useful lemmas that will help bound the gradients / lipschitz constants of various quantities of interest.

lemma e.1 (norm and perturbation bounds). the following bounds hold for  $t > n$ :

(a)  $\| \mathsf{d}\mathbf{v}_t^{\mathrm{pred}}\|_{\mathrm{op}}\leq 2\sqrt{m} r_{\mathrm{nat}}r_{g_\star}$  
(b)  $\| \mathbf{v}_t^{\mathrm{pred}}(m) - \mathbf{v}_t^\star (m)\| _2\leq 3\sqrt{m} r_{\mathcal{m}}r_{\mathrm{nat}}r_{g_\star}\| m\|_{\mathrm{f}}$

(c)  $\| \mathsf{d}(\mathbf{v}_t^{\mathrm{pred}} - \widehat{\mathbf{v}}_t)\|_{\mathrm{op}}\leq 2\sqrt{m}\epsilon_g r_{\mathrm{nat}}$  
(d) for all  $m \in \mathcal{m}$ ,  $\| \mathbf{v}_t^{\mathrm{pred}}(m) - \widehat{\mathbf{v}}_t(m)\|_2 \leq 5r_{\mathcal{m}}r_{\mathrm{nat}}\epsilon_g$ .

proof. note that operator norm bounds on the differential are equivalent to the frobenius-to- $\ell_2$  lipschitz constants of the associated mappings. the proofs are then analogous to the proof of lemma d.2, where the role of  $\mathbf{y}_t^{\mathrm{nat}}$  and  $g_{\star}$  are replaced with the appropriate quantities. for clarity, we provide a relevant generalization of that lemma, without proof.

lemma e.2 (lipschitz bound on generalized coordinate mappings). let  $\widetilde{g},\widetilde{\mathbf{y}}_{1:t}^{\mathrm{nat}},\widetilde{\mathbf{u}}_{1:t}^{\mathrm{nat}}$  be arbitrary, let  $r_{\widetilde{g}} = \| \widetilde{g}\|_{\ell_1,\mathrm{op}}$  and  $\widetilde{r}_{\mathrm{nat},t} \coloneqq \max \left\{\|\widetilde{\mathbf{y}}_s^{\mathrm{nat}}\| : s \in [t - h - m + 1 : t]\right\}$ . then,

$$
\begin{array}{l} \left\| \mathbf {v} _ {t} (m \mid \widetilde {g}, \widetilde {\mathbf {v}} _ {t} ^ {\mathrm {n a t}}, \widetilde {\boldsymbol {\eta}} _ {1: t} ^ {\mathrm {n a t}}) - \mathbf {v} _ {t} (\tilde {m} \mid \widetilde {g}, \widetilde {\mathbf {v}} _ {t} ^ {\mathrm {n a t}}, \widetilde {\boldsymbol {\eta}} _ {1: t} ^ {\mathrm {n a t}}) \right\| _ {2} \\ \leq \widetilde {r} _ {\mathrm {n a t}, t} r _ {\widetilde {g}} \| m - \tilde {m} \| _ {\ell_ {1}, \mathrm {o p}} \leq \sqrt {m} r _ {\widetilde {g}} \widetilde {r} _ {\mathrm {n a t}, t} \| m - \tilde {m} \| _ {\mathrm {f}}. \\ \end{array}
$$

the generalized to non-unary functions of  $m_{t - h:t}$  is analogous lemma d.2. notice the above bound does not depend on  $\widetilde{\mathbf{v}}_t^{\mathrm{nat}}$ , which constitutes an affine term.

. for part (a), the bound follows by bounding  $\| \widehat{\eta}_t^{\mathrm{nat}}\| \leq 2r_{\mathrm{nat}}$  by lemma 6.1b, and applying lemma e.2 with  $\widetilde{\eta}_t^{\mathrm{nat}}\gets \widehat{\eta}_t^{\mathrm{nat}}$ , and  $\widetilde{\mathbf{v}}_t^{\mathrm{nat}}\gets \widehat{\mathbf{v}}_t^{\mathrm{nat}}$ , and  $\widetilde{g}\gets g_{\mathrm{ex}\to (y,u)}$ . {ms: from here}

in part (b), we can compute

$$
\begin{array}{l} \| \mathbf {v} _ {t} ^ {\mathrm {p r e d}} (m) - \mathbf {v} _ {t} ^ {\star} (m) \| _ {2} \leq \| \mathbf {v} _ {t} (m \mid g _ {\mathrm {e x} \rightarrow (y, u)}, \mathbf {v} _ {t} ^ {\mathrm {n a t}}, \widehat {\boldsymbol {\eta}} _ {1: t} ^ {\mathrm {n a t}}) - \mathbf {v} _ {t} (m \mid g _ {\mathrm {e x} \rightarrow (y, u)}, \mathbf {v} _ {t} ^ {\mathrm {n a t}}, \widehat {\boldsymbol {\eta}} _ {1: t} ^ {\mathrm {n a t}}) \| _ {2} \\ = \left\| \mathbf {v} _ {t} \big (m \mid g _ {\mathrm {e x} \rightarrow (y, u)}, \mathbf {v} _ {t} ^ {\mathrm {n a t}}, \widehat {\boldsymbol {\eta}} _ {1: t} ^ {\mathrm {n a t}} - \widehat {\boldsymbol {\eta}} _ {1: t} ^ {\mathrm {n a t}} \big) \right\| _ {2}. \\ \end{array}
$$

let us apply lemma e.2 with  $\widetilde{\eta}_t^{\mathrm{nat}}\gets \eta_t^{\mathrm{nat}} - \widehat{\eta}_t^{\mathrm{nat}}$  and  $\widetilde{g}\gets g_{\mathrm{ex}\to (y,u)}$ . the associated value of  $\widetilde{r}_{\mathrm{nat},t}$  can be replaced by an upper bound on  $\max \{\pmb {\eta}_t^{\mathrm{nat}} - \widehat{\pmb{\eta}}_t^{\mathrm{nat}}:s\in [n + 1:t]\}$ , which we can take to be  $3r_{\mathcal{m}}r_{\mathrm{nat}}\epsilon_g$  by lemma 6.4b. this gives a bound of  $3\sqrt{m} r_{\mathcal{m}}r_{\mathrm{nat}}r_{g_*}\| m\|_{\mathrm{f*}}$ , as needed.

for part (c), take  $\widetilde{\eta}_t^{\mathrm{nat}}\gets \widehat{\eta}_t^{\mathrm{nat}}\widetilde{g}\gets \widehat{g}_{\mathrm{ex}\to (y,u)} - g_{\mathrm{ex}\to (y,u)}$  playing the role of  $g_{\star}$ , yielding a  $\widetilde{r}_{\mathrm{nat},t}\leq 2r_{\mathrm{nat}}$  by lemma 6.1b and  $r_{\widetilde{g}}\leq \epsilon_g$  from lemma d.5.

finally, let us establish part (d). we have

$$
\begin{array}{l} \| \mathbf {v} _ {t} ^ {\mathrm {p r e d}} (m) - \widehat {\mathbf {v}} _ {t} (m) \| _ {2} \leq \| \mathbf {v} _ {t} ^ {\mathrm {n a t}} - \widehat {\mathbf {v}} _ {t} ^ {\mathrm {n a t}} \| _ {2} + \| \sum_ {i = 0} ^ {h} (g _ {\mathrm {e x} \rightarrow (y, u)} ^ {[ i ]} - \widehat {g} _ {\mathrm {e x} \rightarrow (y, u)} ^ {[ i ]}) \mathbf {u} _ {t - i} ^ {\mathrm {e x}} (m | \widehat {\boldsymbol {\eta}} _ {1: t - i} ^ {\mathrm {n a t}}) \| \\ \leq \| \mathbf {v} _ {t} ^ {\mathrm {n a t}} - \widehat {\mathbf {v}} _ {t} ^ {\mathrm {n a t}} \| _ {2} + \| \sum_ {i = 0} ^ {h} (g _ {\mathrm {e x} \rightarrow (y, u)} ^ {[ i ]} - \widehat {g} _ {\mathrm {e x} \rightarrow (y, u)} ^ {[ i ]}) \mathbf {u} _ {t - i} ^ {\mathrm {e x}} (m | \widehat {\boldsymbol {\eta}} _ {1: t - i} ^ {\mathrm {n a t}}) \| \\ \leq \| \mathbf {v} _ {t} ^ {\mathrm {n a t}} - \widehat {\mathbf {v}} _ {t} ^ {\mathrm {n a t}} \| _ {2} + 2 r _ {\mathcal {m}} r _ {\mathrm {n a t}} \sum_ {i = 0} ^ {h} \| (g _ {\mathrm {e x} \rightarrow (y, u)} ^ {[ i ]} - \widehat {g} _ {\mathrm {e x} \rightarrow (y, u)} ^ {[ i ]}) \| _ {\mathrm {o p}} \\ \leq \left\| \mathbf {v} _ {t} ^ {\mathrm {n a t}} - \widehat {\mathbf {v}} _ {t} ^ {\mathrm {n a t}} \right\| _ {2} + 2 r _ {\mathcal {m}} r _ {\mathrm {n a t}} \epsilon_ {g}, \\ \end{array}
$$

where the second to last step uses lemma d.5. finally, we can bound  $\| \mathbf{v}_t^{\mathrm{nat}} - \hat{\mathbf{v}}_t^{\mathrm{nat}}\| _2\leq 3r_{\mathcal{m}}r_{\mathrm{nat}}\epsilon_g$  by lemma 6.4b. combining the bounds yields the proof.

# e.2.2 gradient error (lemmas 8.1 and 8.1b)

lemma 8.1b. for any  $m \in \mathcal{m}$ , we have that

$$
\left\| \nabla f _ {t} (m \mid \widehat {g}, \widehat {\mathbf {y}} _ {1: t} ^ {\mathrm {n a t}}, \widehat {\mathbf {u}} _ {1: t} ^ {\mathrm {n a t}}) - \nabla f _ {t} ^ {\mathrm {p r e d}} (m) \right\| _ {\mathrm {f}} \leq c _ {\mathrm {a p p r o x}} \epsilon_ {g},
$$

where is  $c_{\mathrm{approx}} \coloneqq \sqrt{m} r_{g_\star} r_{\mathcal{m}} r_{\mathrm{nat}}^2 (8\beta_{\mathrm{loss}} + 12l)$ .

proof. let denote the differential of the functions as maps from  $\mathbb{r}^{(md_yd_u)}$ , respectively. define differentials analogously for  $\mathbf{u}^{\mathrm{pred}}, \mathbf{y}^{\mathrm{pred}}$ . then,

$$
\begin{array}{l} \nabla f _ {t} ^ {\mathrm {p r e d}} (m) - \nabla f _ {t} (m \mid \widehat {g} _ {\mathrm {e x} \rightarrow (y, u)}, \widehat {\boldsymbol {\eta}} _ {1: t} ^ {\mathrm {n a t}}, \widehat {\mathbf {v}} _ {t} ^ {\mathrm {n a t}}) = \underbrace {\mathsf {d} \mathbf {v} _ {t} ^ {\mathrm {p r e d}} \cdot \left((\nabla \ell_ {t}) (\mathbf {v} _ {t} ^ {\mathrm {p r e d}} (m)) - (\nabla \ell_ {t}) (\widehat {\mathbf {v}} _ {t} (m))\right)} _ {(a)} \\ + \underbrace {\mathsf {d} (\mathbf {v} _ {t} ^ {\mathrm {p r e d}} - \widehat {\mathbf {v}} _ {t}) (\nabla \ell_ {t}) (\widehat {\mathbf {v}} _ {t} (m))} _ {(b)} \\ \end{array}
$$

we can bound the first term via

$$
\begin{array}{l} \| (a) \| _ {\mathrm {o p}} \stackrel {(i)} {\leq} \left\| \mathsf {d} \mathbf {v} _ {t} ^ {\mathrm {p r e d}} (m) \right\| _ {\mathrm {o p}} \cdot \beta_ {\mathrm {l o s s}} \cdot \left\| \mathbf {v} _ {t} ^ {\mathrm {p r e d}} (m) - \widehat {\mathbf {v}} _ {t} (m) \right\| _ {\mathrm {o p}} \\ \stackrel {(i i)} {\leq} \left(2 \sqrt {m} r _ {g _ {\star}} r _ {\mathrm {n a t}}\right) \cdot \beta_ {\mathrm {l o s s}} \cdot \left(4 r _ {\mathcal {m}} r _ {\mathrm {n a t}} \epsilon_ {g}\right) \\ = 8 \beta_ {\mathrm {l o s s}} \sqrt {m} r _ {g _ {\star}} r _ {\mathcal {m}} r _ {\mathrm {n a t}} ^ {2} \epsilon_ {g} \\ \end{array}
$$

where  $(i)$  uses smoothness of the loss,  $(ii)$  uses lemma e.1. to bound term  $(b)$ , we use the lipschitzness from assumption 2 to bound the norm of the gradient:

$$
\| (\nabla \ell) (\widehat {\mathbf {v}} _ {t} (m)) \| _ {2} \leq l \max  \{1, \| \widehat {\mathbf {v}} _ {t} (m) \| \} \leq 6 l r _ {g _ {\star}} r _ {\mathcal {m}} r _ {\text {n a t}} \quad (\text {b y})
$$

hence, from lemma e.1,

$$
\begin{array}{l} \| (b) \| _ {\mathrm {o p}} \leq 6 l r _ {g _ {\star}} r _ {\mathcal {m}} r _ {\mathrm {n a t}} \cdot \left\| \mathsf {d} (\mathbf {v} _ {t} ^ {\mathrm {p r e d}} - \widehat {\mathbf {v}} _ {t}) \right\| _ {\mathrm {o p}} \\ \leq 6 l r _ {g _ {*}} r _ {\mathcal {m}} r _ {\text {n a t}} \cdot 2 \sqrt {m} \epsilon_ {g} r _ {\text {n a t}} = 1 2 \sqrt {m} l r _ {g _ {*}} r _ {\text {n a t}} ^ {2} r _ {\mathcal {m}} \epsilon_ {g} \\ \end{array}
$$

hence, we conclude that

$$
\| \nabla f _ {t} ^ {\mathrm {p r e d}} (m) - \nabla f _ {t} (m \mid \widehat {g}, \widehat {\mathbf {y}} _ {1: t} ^ {\mathrm {n a t}}, \widehat {\mathbf {u}} _ {1: t} ^ {\mathrm {n a t}}) \| \leq \underbrace {\sqrt {m} r _ {g _ {\star}} r _ {\mathcal {m}} r _ {\mathrm {n a t}} ^ {2} (8 \beta_ {\mathrm {l o s s}} + 1 2)} _ {:= c _ {\mathrm {a p p r o x}}} \cdot \epsilon_ {g}
$$

# e.2.3 smoothness, strong convexity, lipschitz (lemmas 8.2, 8.3/8.3b, and 8.4)

we begin by checking verifying the smoothness bound, which we recall from section 8.1:

lemma 8.2. under condition 6.1,  $f_{t}^{\mathrm{pred}}(m)$  are  $4\beta_{f}$ -smooth, for  $\beta_{f}$  as in lemma 7.1.

proof. the proof follows by modifying lemma 7.1b, replaced  $\mathsf{d}\mathbf{v}_t^\star$  with  $\mathsf{d}\mathbf{v}_t^{\mathrm{pred}}$ . by lemma e.1 part (a), we bound the operator norm of these differentials by twice the corresponding bound in lemma 7.1b, incurring an additional factor of four in the final result.

next, we check lipschitznes:

lemma 8.4 (lipschitzness: unknown & strongly convex). recall the lipschitz constant  $l_{f}$  from lemma 5.4. then under condition 6.1,  $f_{t}^{\mathrm{pred}}(m)$  is  $4l_{f}$ -lipschitz,  $f_{t}^{\mathrm{pred}}[m_{t:t - h}]$  is  $4l_{f}$  coordinate lipschitz. moreover,  $\max_{m\in \mathcal{m}}\| \nabla \widehat{f}_t(m;\widehat{g},\widehat{\mathbf{y}}_{1:t}^{\mathrm{nat}})\| _2\leq 4l_f$ .

proof of lemma 8.4. we prove the general stabilized case. recall that for the known-system setting, the losses  $f_{t}(|g_{\mathrm{ex}\rightarrow (y,u)},\pmb{\eta}_{1:t}^{\mathrm{nat}},\mathbf{v}_{t}^{\mathrm{nat}})$  and  $f_{t}[||g_{\mathrm{ex}\rightarrow (y,u)},\pmb{\eta}_{1:t}^{\mathrm{nat}},\mathbf{v}_{t}^{\mathrm{nat}}]$  are  $l_{f} \coloneqq l\sqrt{m} r_{\mathrm{nat}}^{2}r_{g_{\star}}^{2}r_{\mathcal{m}}$ -lipschitz and  $l_{f}$ -coordinate lipschitz, respectively. under condition 6.1b, we have that  $\| \widehat{g}_{\mathrm{ex}\rightarrow (y,u)}\|_{\ell_1,\mathrm{op}} \leq 2r_{g_\star}$ , and moreover, by lemma 6.1b, we have that  $\| \widehat{\mathbf{v}}_s^{\mathrm{nat}}\|_2, \| \widehat{\pmb{\eta}}_s^{\mathrm{nat}}\|_2 \leq 2r_{\mathrm{nat}}$  for all  $s \in [t]$ . hence, repeating the computation of the known-system lipschitz constant in lemma 5.4, but with inflated norms of  $\widehat{\mathbf{u}}_{1:t}^{\mathrm{nat}}$  adn  $\widehat{\mathbf{y}}_{1:t}^{\mathrm{nat}}$ , we find that  $f_{t}^{\mathrm{pred}}$  (resp.  $f_{t}^{\mathrm{pred}}$ ) are  $\overline{l}_f \coloneqq 4l_f$ -lipschitz (resp. -coordinate lipschitz).

finally, we verify strong convexity in this setting. the following subsumes lemma 8.3:

lemma 8.3b (strong convexity: unknown stabilized system). consider the stabilized setting, with  $\alpha_{f;m}$  as in proposition 7.1b. suppose further that the conditions of that proposition hold, and in addition,

$$
\epsilon_ {g} \leq \frac {1}{9 r _ {\mathcal {m}} r _ {\mathrm {n a t}} r _ {g _ {\star}}} \sqrt {\frac {\alpha_ {f ; m}}{m \alpha_ {\mathrm {l o s s}}}}
$$

then, the functions are  $f_{t;k}^{\mathrm{pred}}$  are  $\alpha_{f;m} / 4$ -strongly convex. analogously, replacing  $\alpha_{f;m}$  by  $\alpha_f$  in the stable setting, the functions  $f_{t;k}^{\mathrm{pred}}$  are  $\alpha_f / 4$  strong convex for  $\alpha_f$  as in proposition 7.1.

proof. let us consider the stabilized case; the stable case is identical. proposition 7.1b (proved in appendix f.2.1) follows from theorem 11, and an can be used to prove the following intermediate bound:

$$
\mathbb {e} \left[ \left\| \mathbf {v} _ {t} (m \mid g _ {\mathrm {e x} \to (y, u)}, \boldsymbol {\eta} _ {1: t} ^ {\mathrm {n a t}}, \mathbf {v} _ {t} ^ {\mathrm {n a t}}) - \mathbf {v} _ {t} ^ {\mathrm {n a t}} \right\| _ {2} ^ {2} \mid \mathcal {f} _ {t - k} \right] \geq \frac {\alpha_ {f ; m}}{\alpha_ {\mathrm {l o s s}}} \| m \| _ {\mathrm {f}} ^ {2}, \quad \forall m = (m ^ {[ i ]}) _ {i = 0} ^ {m - 1}
$$

to deduce our desired strong convexity bound, it suffices to show that  $m = (m^{[i]})_{i=0}^{m-1}$ ,

$$
\mathbb {e} [ \| \mathbf {v} _ {t} ^ {\mathrm {p r e d}} (m) - \mathbf {v} _ {t} ^ {\mathrm {n a t}} \| _ {2} ^ {2} | \mathcal {f} _ {t - k} ] \geq \frac {\alpha_ {f ; m}}{4 \alpha_ {\ell}} \| m \| _ {\mathrm {f}} ^ {2},
$$

with an additional slack factor of  $1/2$ . to begin, note the elementary inequality

$$
\begin{array}{l} \| v + w \| _ {2} ^ {2} = \| v \| _ {2} ^ {2} + \| w \| _ {2} ^ {2} - 2 \| v \| \| w \| \geq \| v \| _ {2} ^ {2} + \| w \| _ {2} ^ {2} - 2 \left(\frac {1}{2} \cdot \frac {1}{2} \| v \| _ {2} ^ {2} + \frac {1}{2} \cdot 2 \| w \| _ {2} ^ {2}\right) \\ = \| v \| _ {2} ^ {2} / 2 - \| w \| _ {2} ^ {2} \\ \end{array}
$$

this yields

$$
\begin{array}{l} \mathbb {e} [ \| \mathbf {v} _ {t} ^ {\mathrm {p r e d}} (m) - \mathbf {v} _ {t} ^ {\mathrm {n a t}} \| _ {2} ^ {2} \mid \mathcal {f} _ {t - k} ] \\ = \mathbb {e} [ \| (\mathbf {v} _ {t} ^ {\mathrm {p r e d}} (m) - \mathbf {v} _ {t} (m | g _ {\mathrm {e x} \rightarrow (y, u)}, \boldsymbol {\eta} _ {1: t} ^ {\mathrm {n a t}}, \mathbf {v} _ {t} ^ {\mathrm {n a t}}) + \mathbf {v} _ {t} (m | g _ {\mathrm {e x} \rightarrow (y, u)}, \boldsymbol {\eta} _ {1: t} ^ {\mathrm {n a t}}, \mathbf {v} _ {t} ^ {\mathrm {n a t}}) - \mathbf {v} _ {t} ^ {\mathrm {n a t}} \| _ {2} ^ {2} | \mathcal {f} _ {t - k} ] \\ \geq \frac {1}{2} \mathbb {e} [ \| \mathbf {v} _ {t} (m \mid g _ {\mathrm {e x} \rightarrow (y, u)}, \boldsymbol {\eta} _ {1: t} ^ {\mathrm {n a t}}, \mathbf {v} _ {t} ^ {\mathrm {n a t}}) - \mathbf {v} _ {t} ^ {\mathrm {n a t}} \| _ {2} ^ {2} \mid \mathcal {f} _ {t - k} ] \\ - \mathbb {e} [ \| \mathbf {v} _ {t} ^ {\mathrm {p r e d}} (m) - \mathbf {v} _ {t} (m \mid g _ {\mathrm {e x} \rightarrow (y, u)}, \boldsymbol {\eta} _ {1: t} ^ {\mathrm {n a t}}, \mathbf {v} _ {t} ^ {\mathrm {n a t}}) \| _ {2} ^ {2} \mid \mathcal {f} _ {t - k} ] \\ \geq \frac {\alpha_ {f ; m} \| m \| _ {\mathrm {f}} ^ {2}}{2 \alpha_ {\ell}} - \underbrace {\mathbb {e} [ \| \mathbf {v} _ {t} ^ {\mathrm {p r e d}} (m) - \mathbf {v} _ {t} (m \mid g _ {\mathrm {e x} \to (y , u)} , \boldsymbol {\eta} _ {1 : t} ^ {\mathrm {n a t}} , \mathbf {v} _ {t} ^ {\mathrm {n a t}}) \| _ {2} ^ {2} \mid \mathcal {f} _ {t - k} ]} _ {(i)}. \\ \end{array}
$$

moreover, by lemma e.1 part (b), we have

$$
\| \mathbf {v} _ {t} ^ {\mathrm {p r e d}} (m) - \mathbf {v} _ {t} (m \mid g _ {\mathrm {e x} \rightarrow (y, u)}, \boldsymbol {\eta} _ {1: t} ^ {\mathrm {n a t}}, \mathbf {v} _ {t} ^ {\mathrm {n a t}}) \| _ {2} ^ {2} \leq (3 r _ {\mathcal {m}} r _ {\mathrm {n a t}} r _ {g _ {\star}} \sqrt {m} \epsilon_ {g}) ^ {2}.
$$

hence, if  $\epsilon_g \leq \frac{\sqrt{\alpha_{f;m} / \alpha_\ell}}{9r_{\mathcal{m}}r_{\mathrm{nat}}r_{g_\star}\sqrt{m}}$ , then the term (i) is bounded by (i)  $\leq \frac{\alpha_{f;m}\|m\|_{\mathrm{f}}^2}{4\alpha_\ell}$ , which concludes the proof.

# e.3 proof of proposition 8.2 (approximation error)

we prove the proposition in the general stabilized setting, where assume the corresponding condition 6.1b holds. recall the set  $\mathcal{m}_0\coloneqq \mathcal{m}(m_0,r_{\mathcal{m}} / 2)$ , and consider a comparator

$$
m _ {\star} \in \arg \inf  _ {m \in \mathcal {m} _ {0}} \sum_ {t = n + m + 2 h + 1} ^ {t} \ell_ {t} \left(\mathbf {y} _ {t} ^ {m}, \mathbf {u} _ {t} ^ {m}\right)
$$

we summarize the conditions of the proposition 8.2 as follows:

condition e.1 (conditions for proposition 8.2). we assume that (a)  $\epsilon_g \leq 1 / r_{\mathcal{m}}$ , (b)  $m \geq 2m_0 + h$ , and (c)  $\psi_{g_\star}(h + 1) \leq r_{g_\star} / t$ .

note that the first condition holds from condition 6.1/6.1b., and the second two from the definition of the algorithm paramaters. the proof has two major steps. we begin with the following claim, which reduces the proof to controlling the differences  $\| \mathbf{u}_t^{\mathrm{ex}}(m_{\mathrm{approx}}\mid \widehat{\mathbf{y}}_{1:t}^{\mathrm{nat}}) - \mathbf{u}_t^{\mathrm{ex}}(m_\star \mid \mathbf{y}_{1:t}^{\mathrm{nat}})\| _2$  between algorithmic inputs on the  $\widehat{\mathbf{y}}^{\mathrm{nat}}$  sequence using  $m_{\mathrm{approx}}$ , and on the  $\mathbf{y}^{\mathrm{nat}}$  sequence using  $m_{\star}$ :

lemma e.3. we have the bound:

$$
\left(\widehat {\mathbf {y}} ^ {\text {n a t}} \text {- a p p r o x e r r o r}\right) \leq 3 l r _ {g _ {\star}} ^ {2} r _ {\mathcal {m}} r _ {\text {n a t}} \sum_ {t = n + m + h + 1} ^ {t} \max  _ {s = t - h} ^ {t} \left\| \mathbf {u} _ {s} ^ {\text {e x}} \left(m _ {\text {a p p r x}} \mid \widehat {\mathbf {y}} _ {1: s} ^ {\text {n a t}}\right) - \mathbf {u} _ {s} ^ {\text {e x}} \left(m _ {\star} \mid \mathbf {y} _ {1: s} ^ {\text {n a t}}\right) \right\| _ {2}
$$

the above lemma is proven in appendix e.3.1.

we will neglect the first  $m_0 + 2h$  terms in the above sum. specifically, defining  $n_{1} = n + m + 3h + 1 + m_{0}$  we have

$$
\begin{array}{l} \left(\widehat {\mathbf {y}} ^ {\text {n a t}} \text {- a p p r o x e r r o r}\right) \leq 3 l r _ {g _ {\star}} ^ {2} r _ {\mathcal {m}} r _ {\text {n a t}} \sum_ {t = n _ {1}} ^ {t} \max  _ {s = t - h} ^ {t} \left\| \mathbf {u} _ {s} ^ {\text {e x}} \left(m _ {\text {a p p r x}} \mid \widehat {\boldsymbol {\eta}} _ {1: s} ^ {\text {n a t}}\right) - \mathbf {u} _ {s} ^ {\text {e x}} \left(m _ {\star} \mid \boldsymbol {\eta} _ {1: s} ^ {\text {n a t}}\right) \right\| _ {2} \\ + 3 l r _ {g _ {\star}} ^ {2} r _ {\mathcal {m}} (m _ {0} + 2 h) \max _ {s = n + m + h + 1} ^ {n _ {1}} \| \mathbf {u} _ {s} ^ {\mathrm {e x}} (m _ {\mathrm {a p p r x}} | \hat {\boldsymbol {\eta}} _ {1: s} ^ {\mathrm {n a t}}) - \mathbf {u} _ {s} ^ {\mathrm {e x}} (m _ {\star} | \boldsymbol {\eta} _ {1: s} ^ {\mathrm {n a t}}) \| _ {2}. \\ \end{array}
$$

moreover, by the triangle inequality and lemmas d.5 and 5.1b

$$
\left\| \mathbf {u} _ {s} ^ {\mathrm {e x}} \left(m _ {\text {a p p r x}} \mid \hat {\boldsymbol {\eta}} _ {1: s} ^ {\mathrm {n a t}}\right) - \mathbf {u} _ {s} ^ {\mathrm {e x}} \left(m _ {\star} \mid \boldsymbol {\eta} _ {1: s} ^ {\mathrm {n a t}}\right) \right\| _ {2} \leq 3 r _ {\mathcal {m}} r _ {\mathrm {n a t}},
$$

giving

$$
\begin{array}{l} \left(\hat {\mathbf {y}} ^ {\text {n a t}} \text {- a p p r o x e r r o r}\right) \leq 3 l r _ {g _ {\star}} ^ {2} r _ {\mathcal {m}} r _ {\text {n a t}} \sum_ {t = n _ {1}} ^ {t} \max  _ {s = t - h} ^ {t} \left\| \mathbf {u} _ {s} ^ {\text {e x}} \left(m _ {\text {a p p r x}} \mid \hat {\boldsymbol {\eta}} _ {1: s} ^ {\text {n a t}}\right) - \mathbf {u} _ {s} ^ {\text {e x}} \left(m _ {\star} \mid \boldsymbol {\eta} _ {1: s} ^ {\text {n a t}}\right) \right\| _ {2} \\ + 9 l \left(m _ {0} + 2 h\right) r _ {g _ {\star}} ^ {2} r _ {\mathcal {m}} ^ {2} r _ {\text {n a t}}. \tag {e.4} \\ \end{array}
$$

we now turn to bounding these  $\mathbf{u}^{\mathrm{ex}}$  differences, which is the main source of difficulty in the proof of proposition 8.2. the next lemma is proven in appendix e.3.2:

lemma e.4. under condition e.1, there exists an  $m_{\mathrm{approx}} \in \mathcal{m}(m,r_{\mathcal{m}})$ , depending only on  $\epsilon_g$  and  $m_{\star}$ , such that for all  $t \geq m + 1$  and  $\tau > 0$ ,

$$
\begin{array}{l} \left\| \mathbf {u} _ {t} ^ {\mathrm {e x}} \left(m _ {\star} \mid \mathbf {y} _ {1: t} ^ {\mathrm {n a t}}\right) - \mathbf {u} _ {t} ^ {\mathrm {e x}} \left(m _ {\text {a p p r x}} \mid \widehat {\mathbf {y}} _ {1: t} ^ {\mathrm {n a t}}\right) \right\| _ {2} \leq \\ \underbrace {\overline {{r}} _ {\mathbf {u}} r _ {\mathcal {m}} \psi_ {g _ {\star}} (h + 1)} _ {\text {(t r u n c a t i o n t e r m)}} + \underbrace {r _ {\mathcal {m}} ^ {2} \epsilon_ {g} ^ {2} \left(\frac {r _ {\mathrm {n a t}} r _ {\mathcal {m}} + \tau^ {- 1}}{2}\right)} _ {\text {(e s t i m a t i o n t e r m)}} + \underbrace {\frac {\tau}{2} \max  _ {j = t - m _ {0} + 1 - h} ^ {t} \left\| \right. \mathbf {u} _ {j} ^ {\mathrm {e x}} \left(\mathbf {m} _ {j} - m _ {\mathrm {a p p r x}}\right) \mid \hat {\boldsymbol {\eta}} _ {1 : j} ^ {\mathrm {n a t}}\left. \right)\left. \right\| _ {2} ^ {2}} _ {\text {(m o v e m e n t t e r m)}} \tag {e.5} \\ \end{array}
$$

from the above lemma and eq. (e.4), and reparametrizing  $\tau \gets 2\tau /3lr_{g_{\star}}^{2}r_{\mathcal{m}}r_{\mathrm{nat}}$ , and bounding  $m_0 + 2h\leq m$ , have

$$
\begin{array}{l} (\widehat {\mathbf {y}} ^ {\text {n a t}} \text {- a p p r o x e r r o r}) \leq 3 l \cdot r _ {\mathcal {m}} ^ {2} r _ {g _ {\star}} ^ {2} r _ {\text {n a t}} \left(t \bar {r} _ {\mathbf {u}} \psi_ {g _ {\star}} (h + 1) + 3 m + r _ {\text {n a t}} r _ {\mathcal {m}} t \epsilon_ {g} ^ {2} \cdot \left(1 + l r _ {g _ {\star}} ^ {2} \tau^ {- 1}\right)\right) \\ + \tau \sum_ {t = n _ {1}} ^ {t} \max _ {j = t - m _ {0} + 1 - h} ^ {t} \left\| \mathbf {u} _ {j} ^ {\mathrm {e x}} (\mathbf {m} _ {j} - m _ {\mathrm {a p p r x}} | \widehat {\boldsymbol {\eta}} _ {1: j} ^ {\mathrm {n a t}}) \right\| _ {2} ^ {2} \\ \end{array}
$$

for  $\psi_{g_{\star}}(h + 1)\leq r_{g_{\star}} / t$  , the above simplifies to

$$
\begin{array}{l} (\widehat {\mathbf {y}} ^ {\mathrm {n a t}} \mathrm {- a p p r o x e r r o r}) \leq 3 l r _ {\mathcal {m}} ^ {3} r _ {g _ {\star}} ^ {2} r _ {\mathrm {n a t}} ^ {2} t \epsilon_ {g} ^ {2} \left(1 + \frac {l r _ {g _ {\star}} ^ {2}}{\tau}\right) + 3 l r _ {\mathcal {m}} ^ {2} r _ {g _ {\star}} ^ {2} r _ {\mathrm {n a t}} (\overline {{r}} _ {\mathbf {u}} r _ {g _ {\star}} + 3 m) \\ + \tau \sum_ {t = n _ {1}} ^ {t} \max _ {j = t - m _ {0} + 1 - h} ^ {t} \left\| \mathbf {u} _ {j} ^ {\mathrm {e x}} (\mathbf {m} _ {j} - m _ {\mathrm {a p p r x}} \mid \widehat {\boldsymbol {\eta}} _ {1: j} ^ {\mathrm {n a t}}) \right\| _ {2} ^ {2} \\ \end{array}
$$

moreover, we can crudely bound

$$
\begin{array}{l} \sum_ {t = n _ {1}} ^ {t} \max  _ {j = t - m _ {0} + 1 - h} ^ {t} \left\| \mathbf {u} _ {j} ^ {\mathrm {e x}} \left(\mathbf {m} _ {j} - m _ {\text {a p p r x}} \mid \hat {\boldsymbol {\eta}} _ {1: j} ^ {\mathrm {n a t}}\right) \right\| _ {2} ^ {2} \leq (m _ {0} + h) \sum_ {t = n _ {1} - m _ {0} - h} ^ {t} \left\| \mathbf {u} _ {j} ^ {\mathrm {e x}} \left(\mathbf {m} _ {j} - m _ {\text {a p p r x}} \mid \hat {\boldsymbol {\eta}} _ {1: j} ^ {\mathrm {n a t}}\right) \right\| _ {2} ^ {2} \\ = (m _ {0} + h) \sum_ {t = n + m + 2 h + 1} ^ {t} \left\| \mathbf {u} _ {j} ^ {\mathrm {e x}} \left(\mathbf {m} _ {j} - m _ {\text {a p p r x}} \mid \hat {\eta} _ {1: j} ^ {\mathrm {n a t}}\right) \right\| _ {2} ^ {2} \\ \end{array}
$$

thus, again reparametrizing  $\tau \gets \tau /(m_0 + h)$ , and bounding  $m_0 + h\leq m$

$$
\begin{array}{l} (\widehat {\mathbf {y}} ^ {\mathrm {n a t}} \mathrm {- a p p r o x e r r o r}) \leq 3 l r _ {\mathcal {m}} ^ {3} r _ {g _ {\star}} ^ {2} r _ {\mathrm {n a t}} ^ {2} t \epsilon_ {g} ^ {2} \left(1 + \frac {l m r _ {g _ {\star}} ^ {2}}{\tau}\right) + 3 l r _ {\mathcal {m}} ^ {2} r _ {g _ {\star}} ^ {2} r _ {\mathrm {n a t}} (\overline {{r}} _ {\mathbf {u}} r _ {g _ {\star}} + 3 m) \\ + \tau \sum_ {t = n + m + h + 1} ^ {t} \left\| \mathbf {u} _ {j} ^ {\mathrm {e x}} \left(\mathbf {m} _ {j} - m _ {\text {a p p r x}} \mid \hat {\boldsymbol {\eta}} _ {1: j} ^ {\mathrm {n a t}}\right) \right\| _ {2} ^ {2}. \tag {e.6} \\ \end{array}
$$

finally, let us upper bound

$$
\begin{array}{l} \left\| \mathbf {u} _ {j} ^ {\mathrm {e x}} \left(\mathbf {m} _ {j} - m _ {\text {a p p r x}} \mid \widehat {\boldsymbol {\eta}} _ {1: j} ^ {\mathrm {n a t}}\right) \right\| _ {2} \leq \max  _ {j = 0} ^ {m - 1} \| \widehat {\boldsymbol {\eta}} _ {j} ^ {\mathrm {n a t}} \| _ {2} \cdot \| \mathbf {m} _ {j} - m _ {\text {a p p r x}} \| _ {\ell_ {1}, \text {o p}} \\ \leq 2 r _ {\mathrm {n a t}} \cdot \| \mathbf {m} _ {j} - m _ {\text {a p p r x}} \| _ {\ell_ {1}, \mathrm {o p}} \quad (\text {l e m m a} 6. 1 \mathrm {b}) \\ \leq 2 r _ {\mathrm {n a t}} \cdot \sqrt {m} \| \mathbf {m} _ {j} - m _ {\text {a p p r x}} \| _ {\mathrm {f}}. \quad \left(\text {l e m m a d . 1}\right) \\ \end{array}
$$

thus,  $\tau \gets \tau /4r_{\mathrm{nat}}^2 m$  , we obtain

$$
\begin{array}{l} \left(\widehat {\mathbf {y}} ^ {\text {n a t}} \text {- a p p r o x e r r o r}\right) \leq 3 l r _ {\mathcal {m}} ^ {3} r _ {g _ {\star}} ^ {2} r _ {\text {n a t}} ^ {2} t \epsilon_ {g} ^ {2} \left(1 + \frac {l m ^ {2} r _ {\text {n a t}} ^ {2} r _ {g _ {\star}} ^ {2}}{\tau}\right) + 3 l r _ {\mathcal {m}} ^ {2} r _ {g _ {\star}} ^ {2} r _ {\text {n a t}} \left(\bar {r} _ {\mathbf {u}} r _ {g _ {\star}} + 3 m\right) \tag {e.7} \\ + \tau \sum_ {t = n + m + h + 1} ^ {t} \| \mathbf {m} _ {j} - m _ {\mathrm {a p p r x}} \| _ {\mathrm {f}} ^ {2}. \\ \end{array}
$$

finally, let us crudely bound the above by

$$
\begin{array}{l} (\widehat {\mathbf {y}} ^ {\text {n a t}} \text {- a p p r o x e r r o r}) \leq 3 6 m ^ {2} r _ {\mathcal {m}} ^ {3} r _ {\text {n a t}} ^ {4} r _ {g _ {\star}} ^ {4} t \epsilon_ {g} \max  \{l, l ^ {2} / \tau \} + 9 l r _ {\mathcal {m}} ^ {2} r _ {g _ {\star}} ^ {2} r _ {\text {n a t}} (\bar {r} _ {\mathbf {u}} r _ {g _ {\star}} + m) \\ + \tau \sum_ {t = n + m + h + 1} ^ {t} \| \mathbf {m} _ {j} - m _ {\mathrm {a p p r x}} \| _ {\mathrm {f}} ^ {2}. \\ \end{array}
$$

as needed.

# e.3.1 proof of lemma e.3

let  $m_{\mathrm{approx}}, m_{\star} \in \mathcal{m}(m, r_{\mathcal{m}})$ , and recall the shorthand

$$
\mathbf {v} _ {t} ^ {\star} (m) := \mathbf {v} _ {t} (m \mid g _ {\mathrm {e x} \rightarrow (y, u)}, \boldsymbol {\eta} _ {1: t} ^ {\mathrm {n a t}}, \mathbf {v} _ {t} ^ {\mathrm {n a t}}), \quad \mathbf {v} _ {t} ^ {\mathrm {p r e d}} (m) := \mathbf {v} _ {t} (m \mid g _ {\mathrm {e x} \rightarrow (y, u)}, \widehat {\boldsymbol {\eta}} _ {1: t} ^ {\mathrm {n a t}}, \mathbf {v} _ {t} ^ {\mathrm {n a t}})
$$

then,

$$
\begin{array}{l} \left| f _ {t} ^ {\text {p r e d}} \left(m _ {\text {a p p r x}}\right) - f _ {t} \left(m _ {\star} \mid g _ {\mathrm {e x} \rightarrow (y, u)}, \boldsymbol {\eta} _ {1: t} ^ {\text {n a t}}, \mathbf {v} _ {t} ^ {\text {n a t}}\right)\right| = \left| \ell_ {t} \left(\mathbf {v} _ {t} ^ {\text {p r e d}} \left(m _ {\text {a p p r x}}\right)\right) - \ell_ {t} \left(\mathbf {v} _ {t} ^ {\star} \left(m _ {\star}\right)\right)\right| \\ \leq l \max \{\underbrace {\| \mathbf {v} _ {t} ^ {\mathrm {p r e d}} (m _ {\mathrm {a p p r x}}) \| _ {2}} _ {(a)}, \underbrace {\| \mathbf {v} _ {t} ^ {\star} (m _ {\star}) \| _ {2} , 1 \}} _ {(b)} \| \mathbf {v} _ {t} ^ {\mathrm {p r e d}} (m _ {\mathrm {a p p r x}}) - \mathbf {v} _ {t} ^ {\star} (m _ {\star}) \| _ {2}. \\ \end{array}
$$

from lemma 5.1b, we have  $(b) \leq 2r_{g_\star}r_\mathcal{m}r_{\mathrm{nat}}$ . moreover, combining with lemma d.5, a similar argument lets us bound  $(a) \leq r_{\mathrm{nat}} + 2r_{g_\star}r_\mathcal{m}r_{\mathrm{nat}} = 3r_{g_\star}r_\mathcal{m}r_{\mathrm{nat}}$ . since these upper bounds are all assumed to be greater than one,

$$
\left| f _ {t} ^ {\text {p r e d}} \left(m _ {\text {a p p r x}}\right) - f _ {t} \left(m _ {\star} \mid g _ {\mathrm {e x} \rightarrow (y, u)}, \boldsymbol {\eta} _ {1: t} ^ {\text {n a t}}, \mathbf {v} _ {t} ^ {\text {n a t}}\right)\right| \leq 3 l r _ {g _ {\star}} r _ {\mathcal {m}} r _ {\text {n a t}} \cdot \| \mathbf {v} _ {t} ^ {\text {p r e d}} \left(m _ {\text {a p p r x}}\right) - \mathbf {v} _ {t} ^ {\star} \left(m _ {\star}\right) \| _ {2}. \tag {e.8}
$$

unfolding

$$
\begin{array}{l} \| \mathbf {v} _ {t} ^ {\mathrm {p r e d}} (m _ {\mathrm {a p p r x}}) - \mathbf {v} _ {t} ^ {\star} (m _ {\star}) \| _ {2} = \left\| \sum_ {i = 0} ^ {h} g _ {\mathrm {e x} \rightarrow (y, u)} ^ {[ i ]} (\mathbf {u} _ {t - i} ^ {\mathrm {e x}} (m _ {\mathrm {a p p r x}} \mid \widehat {\boldsymbol {\eta}} _ {1: t - i} ^ {\mathrm {n a t}}) - \mathbf {u} _ {t - i} ^ {\mathrm {e x}} (m _ {\star} \mid \boldsymbol {\eta} _ {1: t - i} ^ {\mathrm {n a t}})) \right\| _ {2} \\ \leq r _ {g _ {\star}} \max _ {i = 0} ^ {h} \left\| \mathbf {u} _ {t - i} ^ {\mathrm {e x}} (m _ {\mathrm {a p p r x}} | \widehat {\boldsymbol {\eta}} _ {1: t - i} ^ {\mathrm {n a t}}) - \mathbf {u} _ {t - i} ^ {\mathrm {e x}} (m _ {\star} | \boldsymbol {\eta} _ {1: t - i} ^ {\mathrm {n a t}})) \right\| _ {2}. \\ \end{array}
$$

combining with eq. (e.8) gives the bound.
